It includes his turn to his next target: he receives facing the thrower and turns through the reception, so it adds no turn of its own. His footwork rule is §5.5's.
- **No early release.** He has no further hurry: his earliest and his set release are both `relay_s` + footwork, at full arm and normal accuracy. The measured relay exchanges are already the quick release of a relay race.
- **Sources** (both `snippet`, mlb.com pages that refuse automated fetches):
  - 0.75 s is an MLB.com analyst's expectation for a big-league shortstop's relay throw (Adames, in "Rays' relay even more amazing under dissection").
  - Grade 70 lands near the one measured elite relay exchange, Tatis Jr.'s "rocket relay" at 0.62 s (MLB.com, the top defensive plays of 2021).
  - No split by relay man or by the base he throws to was found.
  - A measured analogue (`snippet`, MLB.com "Statcast adds pop time, exchange, arm strength"): Statcast's catcher exchange, glove to release, has a league mean of 0.73 s (elite 0.64 s, poor 0.85 s). A catcher already faces his target; a relay man turns half round. So 0.75 s for a relay is, if anything, quick.
- **Why.** Before, he was set at 0.7 × `Transfer` (0.455 s) and could hurry to 0.6 of that (0.27 s). Measured relays home took 0.28–0.38 s, quicker than the quickest relay on record. When every tagging runner was sent from third, those relays decided most races at 250–300 ft.
- **What the players know.** League knowledge follows the truth:
  - the holder's own estimate of a relay route (`throw_race`, §5.6) uses his cutoff man's `relay_s`, from his `Transfer` (known to his teammates);
  - a runner's read of a relay route behind a held ball (`read_throw_time`, `typical_throw_time`) uses the league's 0.75 s, where it used 0.7 × 0.75 = 0.525 s;
  - a runner reads a relay man's `relay_s`, from that position's grade-50 `Transfer`, only for a reception he can tell is a relay (§6.4): the man visibly showed the cutoff job, or took it at least `RELAY_SEEN_M` = 6 m [E] from every bag (base receivers took throws up to 6 m off a bag in 100 traced games; Opus relay review P3-1). A base receiver pulled a few metres off his bag is read as a feed, 0.7 of `Transfer` and his turn. The first build read every reception more than 1.5 m from a bag as a relay;
  - his belief of what a cut costs (`CUT_EXTRA_S`, §6.3) rises by the same 0.15 s, to 1.05 s: it was "the relay man's 0.6 s and the longer way round".
  - *Known and deferred* (Opus relay review P3-2): a runner's read of a throw **in flight** to the cutoff man (`ThrownRead::time_to`, §6.4) still takes it to the nearest base, then `RELAY_READ_S` (0.6 s) and a typical throw from that bag. For a throw to a relay man 55 m out, that is about 0.6 s earlier than the world (about 0.2 s before this change). With relays now rare (about one in 100 games; throw-race note §8) no runner decision in 100 traced games fell inside a relay throw. If relays come back, give the read the relay man's spot and `RELAY_S_50` when the throw comes down well short of every bag.
- *Measured (2026-11-14, `dp_probe`, seeds 0–255):* pivot catch-to-release 0.75 s (it was 0.43 s); contact to the ball at first on completed pivots 4.31 s (real 4.0–4.5 s); grounder double plays 15.3% per opportunity (21.0% before, 9.8% real). See docs/calibration/2026-11-14-dp-pivot.md.

### 5.6 Throw decision

At the end of the transfer, the fielder evaluates each option (each base with a runner heading to it, the cutoff, hold the ball):

```
P̂(out) = Φ( (t̂_runner − t̂_ball) / σ_FIQ )
value  = P̂(out) · ΔRE_out + (1 − P̂(out)) · ΔRE_safe  − throw-error risk term
```

- `t̂_runner`, `t̂_ball`: the fielder's own estimates (runner speed estimated from observation; ball time from the agent's throw model).
- σ_FIQ from `FieldingIQ` (§10). ΔRE from the run-expectancy table (§8).
- Pick the best option; ties broken by a fixed option order.

**A4 implementation contract (2026-11-14; measuring note priorities 2, 1, 3).** The interim p² × w model below remains in use; this change does not introduce score/run-expectancy or throw-error value, change `TWO_OUT_TAG_W`, or tune the run environment.

- **Visible targets before routes.** An out-attempt destination is built from the runner's current public path position and **signed current velocity**, as the fielder reads them. The runner's private intended destination is never a decision input. The world ends a stopping runner's physical speed on the arrival tick (preserving a batter's protected first-base overrun), rather than leaving arrival speed live for another tick. A runner within `on_bag_m` = 0.05 m [E] of a bag is not projected to the following bag: the holder waits until he visibly leaves it. Below `moving_mps` = 0.5 m/s [E] there is no moving-runner out option. Between bags, positive motion targets the next bag and negative motion the preceding one. Arrival at third and a retreat to third therefore cannot generate an out attempt at home. Passing through third and visibly leaving it can. Pending force/appeal touches and immediate reachable tags remain before this screen. The current interim runner read has no added perception noise; it reads public position and velocity, not internal plans. A future noisy reader must feed these same quantities into the target builder.
- **One common comparison.** For every admitted runner destination, evaluate a direct/relay throw and (when `carry_max_m > 0`) a carry (within `carry_max_m`, or from further away when carrying completes sooner than throwing to the covering receiver, so a first baseman fielding near his 11 m Savant `Standard` spot does not stall waiting for the pitcher; a force touch or a tag plan, §5.5). Use each action's own completion time in the existing Φ(.../0.12 s), p > 0.3, p² × w comparison. Waiting for get-up, receiver cover and transfer belongs to the appropriate action, never a reason to take a carry before comparing the other bases. Values equal to 1e-9 go to a carry over a throw, whichever runner each is for, then to the larger margin: avoid an unnecessary throw when an out is equally secure (fixed 2026-11-14, Gemini A4 review P1-1: the first exact tie was retained, so with two out a throw to first, listed first, beat a force a step away; p saturates to 1.0 for a second or more in hand). Re-evaluate while holding; a newly stopped runner invalidates the destination before a route can be chosen or released. An abandoned carry brakes in place unless a new job replaces it; a holder who was not carrying keeps his target (a receiver still steps to his bag).
- **Bounded double-play continuation.** With fewer than two outs, a first **force** beyond first may leave the batter forced at first. Add `continuation_weight` = 1.0 [E] × p_first² × p_second² × w_second to its existing first-out value. The second probability uses the still-running batter's observed ETA to first, the first action's completion, the prospective holder's own throw speed, the first-base cover time, and the existing 0.05 s handling allowance. A receiver needs his existing 0.7 × transfer; a carrier retains setup time already spent holding the ball. w_second is the existing first-base weight with one additional out (0.9 below two outs; 1.0 for the inning-ending force). A first out on the batter removes forces and earns no such bonus; an unforced tag earns no force continuation; two outs already means no bonus. This is one further force out, not a speculative sequence or a new score/situation model. Probabilities are beliefs only; outcomes still come solely from physical facts. The continuation counts only when p_second > 0.3, the threshold at which the second throw would be attempted (2026-11-14, Gemini A4 review P1-2).
- **Configuration.** `bb_agents::throwing::ChoiceParams`, carried in `FielderAttrs::throw_choice`, holds the named [E] thresholds above and the continuation weight. Defaults are the values printed here. They are exposed for fixtures/experiments, not adjusted to restore any aggregate rate.
- **Independent cases.** A runner stopped on third offers no home attempt; a runner retreating between third and second offers second, regardless of private intention. A sure force home beats carrying first when carrying removes all further forces (`23-109` bases-loaded fixture: `carry 1` is admitted, loses to throwing home, and records the `[4, 1]` double play). A sure throw-second/throw-first double play beats a single home tag with `continuation_weight = 1.0` (`3-140` 1st-and-3rd fixture, flipping to home at `continuation_weight = 0.0`), while constructed unit tests verify the carry-first `force_continuation` branch and the 0-out `w_second = 0.9` weight (`carry_double_play_at_second_uses_carrier_continuation_and_out_weight`, `carry_non_second_force_dp_uses_carrier_post_touch_setup_clock`); continuation is zero with two outs. A third baseman must not run toward third for a runner who has stopped at second (seed 17/play 203 fixture); for an unforced runner visibly coming, two out takes the sure force at first and fewer than two out the sure tag of the lead runner at third (constructed test). Seed 1/play 64 must lose the fictitious home attempt. Fixtures freeze the original contact and public starting situation from main so earlier changed pitches cannot move the cases.

### 5.7 Defensive coordination

**A4 destination gate (2026-11-14):** moving-runner targets in cover/relay coordination use the same public position/signed-velocity screen as §5.6. A stopped runner on third never raises a relay's moving-runner target to home. The existing starting-occupancy assignment table still prepares a cutoff toward a likely play; this is not itself an instruction to throw there. The holder admits an actual moving destination **before** comparing direct and cutoff routes. With no admitted out attempt, the existing return-to-second/hold policy remains. No cutoff route-time parameters or two-out weights change.

**Runner defaults.** `RunnerIn::speed` and `RunnerIn::tau_s` carry the player’s movement inputs. The defaults are 8.2 m/s and τ = 0.76 s (`LEAGUE_TOP_SPEED_MPS` and `LEAGUE_TAU_S` in `sim/crates/bb_agents/src/running.rs`), for the owner’s 4.10 s standing 90-ft target. The batter starts 0.20 s after contact (`BATTER_START`). `LEAD_M` sets the other runners’ leads to 5.5, 6.5 and 4.5 m off first, second and third. See §6.2–§6.4 for movement, decisions and tag-ups.

**Backups (built 2026-11-11, `Sim::assign_backups`):** every fielder with no other job backs up the play: one behind the man fielding the ball and one behind each base a throw is likely to go to, on the line of that throw and about 13 m beyond the base, never two at one base. Who goes where follows standard assignments (the pitcher behind third or home, the far outfielder behind second, the catcher behind first with the bases empty); the list was first written up in docs/calibration/2026-11-11-owner-watch-notes.md, and the table below is the current one. Each base job lists its candidates in order of preference, and the first one still free takes it:

| Ball | Bases | Behind the fielder | Behind second | Behind third | Behind home | Behind first |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Outfield (fielded > `INFIELD_R` = 47.40 m from home, the infield dirt's edge, rules-spec §5.8) | empty | nearest free outfielder | far-side outfielder, else CF | **pitcher** (owner, 2026-11-13) | — (the catcher has left home) | catcher, on the line from second |
| Outfield | runner on first | nearest free outfielder | far-side outfielder, else CF | pitcher (from foul ground), else LF | — (the catcher covers home) | — |
| Outfield | runner on second or third | nearest free outfielder | far-side outfielder, else CF | — | pitcher | — |
| Infield | any | nearest free outfielder | CF, else far-side outfielder (runner on first only) | LF, else pitcher (runner on second only) | — | catcher, else RF |

The far-side outfielder is the left fielder on a ball to the right side (x > 0) and the right fielder otherwise. Bases-empty outfield ball, pitcher behind third: **the owner's decision of 2026-11-13** (docs/calibration/2026-11-12-pitcher-outfield-diagnosis.md, Part B). On a ball in the gap or down a line the batter-runner may try for three, and third is the base the throw would go to after second; the pitcher's other standard place, behind home, has no runner to play. Before this the pitcher was a fallback for second and almost never got a job, so he stood near the mound (after Part A) or ran toward the ball (before it). When the throw is actually released, `back_up_throw` moves the backup of that base onto the real throw's line; if nobody has that job, the nearest fielder with nothing to do takes it. **A fielder with no job (`Role::Idle`) does not chase the ball (2026-11-13):** whenever the defense calls or re-calls the ball, a fielder left without a chase, cover, cutoff or backup job pulls up where braking stops him (`stop_point`: v²/(2·`BRAKE`) along his motion) and stands there until he is given a job. The same holds for every other way a job ends (Codex idle-fielder review #1, `go_idle`): a cutoff or relay man whose job is over (a throw goes past him, or an infielder has the ball), a dropped backup, and the bases-empty catcher leaving home all brake to a stop; any real cover or backup job is then given explicitly. This happens once, when the job ends, not every tick. Before this, `cover_roles` left in his target the intercept every candidate computes during the call, so idle fielders, most visibly the pitcher on bases-empty fly balls, kept sprinting toward the ball (docs/calibration/2026-11-12-pitcher-outfield-diagnosis.md, Part A). That drift had also been an unplanned safety net: an infielder called off a blooper kept running and caught the ones the new chaser missed. Without it more shallow flies and pop-ups drop (docs/calibration/2026-11-13-idle-fielder-target.md). **Peeling off (built 2026-11-13, `PEEL_BACKUP_M` = 5 m [E]):** an infielder (pitcher and catcher included) who believed he could catch a ball in the air, and hears a sure call by someone else, does not stop: unless a cover job takes him, he backs up the catch from `PEEL_BACKUP_M` short of where *his own* read has it coming down, on the side he is coming from (out of the caller's way and out of reach, close enough to field a ball that drops or pops out). While the call stands he makes no attempt to catch the ball, however close it comes (his read may be off, or he may still be backing away). He keeps the job across re-calls he is not part of; it ends, checked every tick, when the ball comes down, is caught or is thrown, and a loose ball is then re-called at once; a ball off a glove is a new ball and a new call. His own read, not the caller's target, because he backs up the ball, not the man: on a shallow pop-up his read is the better one. Since he is now near the ball, a re-call can hand it back to him when the caller's read falls apart (seed 29 play 135: the centre fielder sure-calls a pop-up he has read 40 m too deep, the shortstop drifts out, and when the centre fielder is no longer sure the shortstop calls it and catches it; before, he stood 20 m away and it dropped). Outfielders called off keep the existing job behind the fielder (deeper, on the ball's line from home).

**Cutoffs and relays (built 2026-11-12, `Sim::relay_job`, `assign_relay`, `update_relay`):** once a ball is down in the outfield more than 50 m from home (or in an outfielder's hands), one infielder lines up between it and the base the throw will likely go to: home with a runner in scoring position, third with a runner on first, otherwise second, raised to any base a runner is visibly heading past that. Toward home the first baseman is the cutoff on a ball the centre or right fielder fields, the third baseman on one the left fielder fields (revised 2026-11-21: before, a ball fielded left of x = −15 m was the third baseman's, and on a ball the centre fielder fielded near that line the job changed hands every 0.3 s; a man known to be out on it keeps it when another outfielder takes the ball over); toward third, the shortstop; toward second, on a ball through the infield on the ground, the middle infielder on its side (below); otherwise, with no runner to play, nobody. *The runners on base here are those on base at the pitch* (revised 2026-11-21): the batter who has reached first on a single does not make the likeliest throw third; it goes to second to hold him, raised only if he is seen going on. (Before, once the batter passed first the shortstop left whatever he was doing to line up toward third.) On a single (not a deep ball) each counts at the base he was on at the pitch unless he stands on third, which threatens home (Gemini A4 review P2-3) (revised again 2026-11-21, Opus liner-single review P2-2; [the relay hand-overs note](calibration/2026-11-22-relay-handovers.md)): a runner from first who has rounded second, or stopped there, is still the runner from first, the throw on him goes to third with the shortstop cutting, and the first baseman stays at first behind the batter-runner; only a runner seen heading home raises it. Counted at the base he had reached, he made the likeliest throw home, and the first baseman went out on the cutoff home with first open until the second baseman saw it (first uncovered on 77 balls with a runner on first in 120 games, now none; counted at the base he had reached once stopped, he stopped at second as the outfielder picked the ball up and sent the corner man out on the cutoff home in the last second before the throw, 58 plays). A corner man known to be out on the cutoff home keeps the throw home on a single while a runner who was on base at the pitch is still on the bases (not out, not scored), moving or not (revised 2026-11-22; with the run in and the runner from first held at second, a real infield does not realign, and holding it only while a runner moves measured worse, [relay hand-overs note](calibration/2026-11-22-relay-handovers.md) §1): with runners on first and third, counted at the pitch's base alone, the runner from first made it third the moment the run from third scored, and the shortstop took the job from the corner man 3.4–3.9 s after contact (11 plays in 120 games; seed 13 play 227). On a deep ball a runner from first going round second on an extra-base hit is the run the relay goes home for. On a deep ball whichever middle infielder (`SS` or `2B`) starts closer in pre-pitch alignment to the relay spot goes out as the relay man instead (and with a play at home the first baseman lines up behind him as the cutoff man home: the double cut, below), early if his own read says he cannot get there before it lands. (This differs from the usual convention, under which the shortstop takes balls to the left of straightaway centre and the second baseman those to the right: against a right-handed batter the second baseman, playing +6.8°, goes out on balls just left of centre. It also uses the pre-pitch spots rather than where the two stand when the job is given, which is just as public. Left as built, Opus infield-fixes review P3-4: low risk, and a change belongs with a measured relay review.) Only a ball an outfielder fields gets one; while the ball is loose, a man whose own read says he gets there before the chaser plays it himself; once a throw goes past him, his job ends: he takes his own next job at once, a base his tables give him, else he is free to back the throw up (revised 2026-11-22, Opus and Codex double-cut reviews P2-1: before, he stopped where he was until the throw was caught; on the double cut that left the first baseman standing with first empty, 241 times in 120 games, 2.6 s on average). **A deep ball** (`deep_ball`, revised twice on 2026-11-21) is one fielded beyond `RELAY_DEEP_M` = 80 m after being hit over or past an outfielder (a ball in an outfielder's hands judged where he secured it, a fixed fact everyone saw, not where he is as he steps in to throw: revised 2026-11-21, Opus liner-single re-review P2-1; a ball taken 80.2 m out stopped being deep under him, and the relay man gave way to the corner man just before the throw, seed 63 play 211): a ball caught, or one that first came down beyond `RELAY_DEEP_M`; one that first came down in the outfield short of that (beyond the infield dirt, `INFIELD_R`) once it is deeper, by more than `PAST_OF_M` = 5 m [E, unsourced: a ball taken within that of his spot came to him; without it a hard liner skipping to the centre fielder at his spot was deep by a metre either way], than the pre-pitch spot of the outfielder whose spot is nearest where it came down (loose, or in a fielder's hands), or, while it is loose and on through the outfielder's pickup to his throw, if a middle infielder was known to be out as its relay man when it came down (as everyone knew it at the touch, a fixed fact: he went out while it was up, on his own read: toward home, or toward third but for the shortstop's cutoff to third with a runner on first) and no corner man was known out on the cutoff home then (with both out the infield's call was split, and the mark alone decides; judged instead on what the relay man was known to be doing now, he stayed out while known out and came back while known back, and with a corner man also out the two traded the job every 0.3 s; held only while it was loose, the fixed mark gave the job at the pickup to the corner man, who left his bag 0.8–2.5 s before the throw: the relay man who is already in line keeps it, re-review P2-1, 6 plays in 120 games to none); or one hit on the ground (first down within the dirt, a fact everyone saw) that has got past the outfielder fielding it: in his hands deeper than his pre-pitch spot (he went back for it), or, still loose, the ball now deeper than both that spot and the man known to be going for it. Once a ball hit on the ground has been judged deep, it stays deep through the outfielder's pickup to his throw (revised 2026-11-22, the double-cut job; Opus relay hand-overs review P3-3): the judgment compares the ball with the spot and position of the outfielder known to be going for it, and when another outfielder takes the ball over from the one it got past, the new man's spot can be deeper than the ball; the call flipped back and the relay man came back in, then went out again (seed 118 play 162: a grounder through the right side past the right fielder, the centre fielder taking it over at 4.26 s and fielding it 114 m out; the second baseman out at 4.11 s, back at 4.26 s, out again at 5.29 s). A ball that has got past one outfielder is past the outfield, whoever runs it down. (Before the liner-single change of 2026-11-21 a ball first down beyond the dirt was deep whenever it was fielded beyond 80 m: a line drive that fell in front of a deep outfielder was a deep ball at first, with a middle infielder out as the relay man for a single, until the outfielder had come in inside 80 m. A first version of this change took a ball first down short of the outfielder's spot as in front of him until it got past him; a gap hit landing short of the outfielders and rolling to the wall then went from the relay man, out while it was up, to the cutoff man at the bounce and back to the relay man as it passed them (an out-back-out on 24 plays with a runner in scoring position in 120 games, against 2 on main). The fixed mark and the known relay man keep it to at most one change. [The liner-single note](calibration/2026-11-21-liner-single-jobs.md).) While the ball is still in the air nothing has come down yet, and it is judged by where it is being fielded alone, as before. (Judged by the fielding point alone, a grounder rolling straight at the centre fielder flickered between deep and not as he started in, and the relay job changed hands.) A ground single fielded in front of the outfielder is not a deep ball, however deep he plays: before this, a grounder the centre fielder (at 97.5 m) charged and took at 88 m counted as deep, so the relay went out for third with nobody on (the owner's saved play 102 of seed 51001). A grounder the outfielder lets by, rolling toward the wall, still gets the relay man. The relay man of a deep ball stands short of the base by the throw's length less 45 m, at least 14 m (about 45 ft) and at most halfway; on a shallower ball the cutoff man stands 14 m (about 45 ft) short of the base, or halfway on a short throw, where a throw through him carries to the base on the fly or one hop (§5.5, revised 2026-11-20). Either keeps to the line as it moves. The fielder with the ball compares, from his throw-time belief, a direct throw with one through the relay man (his throw, the relay man's quick release, the relay man's throw) and takes the quicker; a throw beyond his reach on the fly always goes through. With no play he gets the ball to second the quicker way. Measured on 512 games (after the review fixes): relays add 0.14–0.15 runs per team-game against none (two seed sets), all of it from the deep relay man and none from routing throws through him; the cause is not yet known (docs/reviews/2026-11-12-codex-throws-relays-review.md, Resolution). The first version, which pulled infielders out while a catchable fly was in the air, doubled hits on pop-ups and was fixed.

**A ground ball through the infield (built 2026-11-21, `through_job`, `second_cutoff_man`; the owner's saved play 102 of seed 51001, [saved-plays note §3](calibration/2026-11-20-saved-plays-51001.md#3-play-102-the-second-baseman-hardly-moved), the Coordinator's Q-C (a); [the mid-infield note](calibration/2026-11-21-mid-infield-jobs.md)).** The standard assignments: on a single through the infield with nobody on, the middle infielder on the ball's side goes out toward the ball as the cutoff man for the throw to second and his partner covers second; with a runner on first the shortstop is the cutoff for third and the second baseman covers second. A middle infielder whose partner is going for the ball stays at or goes to second, and a shortstop with a runner on first holds second for the force until the ball is plainly through (Opus mid-infield review P1-1, P2-2, 2026-11-21).
- *The cutoff to second.* With no runner on base at the pitch beyond what sends the throw elsewhere (the likeliest throw is second), on a ball hit on the ground (first down within `INFIELD_R`) that an outfielder fields, the cutoff man is the middle infielder on the side the ball is on as everyone sees it (`ball_side_x`: where it is being fielded once that is known, else where it is heading), the shortstop on the left (x < 0), the second baseman on the right. That is the side the coverage table uses for second, so his partner is the man it sends to second. A partner already known to be out on that cutoff keeps it, so the job does not change hands when a ball up the middle is later seen on the other side of second; more exactly (revised later on 2026-11-21), the one first known to have gone out on it on this ball keeps it, whatever he and his partner are known to be doing since (on a blooper up the middle whose side changed, seed 6 play 233, each in turn saw the other out 0.3 s late and handed it back, every 0.3 s for 1.9 s). He stands at the cutoff spot of a throw through him (`through_spot`). Since the liner-single change (2026-11-21) the same holds on a line-drive or fly single, a ball that came down before anyone caught it (below, "A ball in the air that will come down in front of an outfielder"); after a catch nobody goes out.
- *At the first call, while no outfielder is known to be going for it* (`through_job`; revised 2026-11-21 after the Opus review, P1-1, P2-1, P2-2). The ball's cutoff man for second (above), or the shortstop when the throw would go to third (a runner on first at the pitch), goes out when all of these hold:
  - the ball has come down within the infield dirt and **his own read** has it on the ground, carrying beyond the infielders' range (`INFIELD_BREAK_R`), with no play for him in the infield (his intercept beyond that range);
  - no infielder he knows to be going for the ball (his chase seen or heard, `CALL_HEARD_S`) is still in front of it: the ball, where everyone sees it now, has not yet gone by him along its path by more than his reach (`REACH`, 1 m);
  - his partner is not known to be going for it at all. *The pair:* the man whose partner is going for the ball stays at, or goes to, second (from the cutoff, or from no job, by the tables), whoever ends up with it; only the 0.3 s before the partner's chase is known can leave both away;
  - by his own estimate of each other infielder the ball has not yet gone by (`seen_intercept`: from the man's visible position and motion, his known speed and reaction, on the cutoff man's own read; whatever job that man is on now) none can field it inside `INFIELD_BREAK_R`. Toward second the pitcher is left out of this estimate, though not of the known chase above: with nobody on there is no force at second, the partner holds the bag, and on a ball up the middle that the pitcher may or may not reach the ball-side man's first steps are toward its line behind second either way (play 102: by the second baseman's estimate the pitcher might reach the ball at 0.93 s; counting him would hold the second baseman still until the ball passed the mound). On a comebacker the pitcher fields cleanly, the man is then out on the job until the catch (measured 2026-11-21: 44 such balls in 120 games, on the cutoff a median 0.18 s and at most 0.30 s before the pitcher had it, which from a set start is under half a metre of running; the figure 0.02 s first given here was the minimum, Opus re-review P3-4). At that cost leaving the pitcher out is still the better call: counting him costs the ball-side man on every ball up the middle about 0.3 s of standing, the owner's complaint on play 102. On 5 of those the shortstop was going for it too, not yet known to the second baseman, and second stood empty for the last 0.1–0.2 s before the pitcher's catch, with nobody on and the play at first. Counting the pitcher would remove these and hold the ball-side man still until the ball passed the mound, about 0.3 s on play 102; not done. Toward third the pitcher counts;
  - toward third, with the force at second at stake, the shortstop also waits until every infielder has shown what he is doing on this ball (`CALL_HEARD_S` after the first call at the soonest).

  Once out, the estimate is not asked again, so the job does not come and go as the estimate crosses the range (the review's flicker). *Toward second* (nobody on) only his partner's chase or a catch takes him off (revised 2026-11-21, Opus re-review P2-1): at the first call nobody's chase is known yet, so he went out on his estimate alone, and a chase that became known 0.3 s later pulled him back, then he went out again once the ball had passed that man (22 such out-back-out plays in 120 games against 8 on main; now 8). *Toward third* (a runner on first) a teammate who becomes known to be going for the ball while it has not yet gone by him (no margin now) also takes him off, back to second for the force. Then he takes his own cover or backup job. He lines up on the line from the base to where his read has the ball going, at the through spot (it is not yet a deep ball: nobody knows it will get past the outfielder). No new parameter: `INFIELD_R`, `INFIELD_BREAK_R` and `REACH` exist. Once an outfielder is known to be going for the ball the ordinary rule above takes over from where he fields it; there too a middle infielder is not given a cutoff to second or third while his partner is known to be going for the ball, nor, while a ground ball is still loose, until the same conditions let him go (`infield_lets_go`: the known chasers, the estimate, and toward third every infielder known); a deep ball's relay man is unchanged. The cutoff to home: below.
- *The second baseman takes second by the convention* (`second_takes_over`). With a runner on first, the second baseman who judges a ground ball through (the same facts and his own read as above: on the dirt, carrying beyond `INFIELD_BREAK_R`, no play for him, no infielder known to be going for it in front of it, the shortstop not known to be going for it, and by his estimate no other infielder the ball has not yet gone by, the pitcher included, can field it in the infield) counts the shortstop as the cutoff man in his own working of the tables and takes second at once, without waiting to see the shortstop go. Once he is on second, only a chase known to him in front of the ball or the shortstop's chase ends that judgment; his read and estimate are not asked again (revised 2026-11-21, Opus re-review P3-1: without the estimate he took second at the first call on balls the first baseman then fielded and dropped to no job 0.3 s later, 56 plays in 120 games with a runner on first against 17 on main; now 16). If he has judged wrong, second has two men on it for a moment.
- *With a runner in scoring position* (built 2026-11-21, `home_cutoff_by_convention`, `through_point`, `through_jobs`, `through_backup`; [the cutoff-home note](calibration/2026-11-21-cutoff-home-jobs.md)). The standard assignments on a single through the infield with a runner on second or third: the cutoff man for the throw home goes out (the first baseman on a ball to the centre or right fielder, the third baseman on one to the left fielder); with the first baseman cutting, the second baseman covers first, the shortstop second and the third baseman third; with the third baseman cutting, the first baseman covers first, the second baseman second and the shortstop third; the catcher stays home and the pitcher backs up home. Real infielders take these jobs as they see the ball go through, from the standard assignments, not by waiting to see the cutoff man go. Before this the cutoff man home went out only once an outfielder was known to be fielding the ball, and the others' tables filled in 0.3 s after that: a middle infielder had no job for 0.6–0.9 s on most such plays (in 120 games, 166 stretches over 0.1 s on 129 ground singles; the second baseman 0.36 s and the shortstop 0.56 s a play), and on a ball to the left of centre the tables put the pitcher at first and left the shortstop with nothing (seed 9 play 9: 3.7 s).
  - *Who will field it, as everyone works it out* (`through_point`): before anyone is known to be going for it, the outfielder whose pre-pitch spot is nearest the ball's ground track as all see it now, at the point of the track nearest that spot (the fielding point's own construction, on a known spot instead of a known chaser). It names the cutoff man home and the line he and the pitcher take.
  - *The cutoff man home goes out at the first call* (`through_job`, toward home) on the same conditions as the shortstop toward third (above): his own read has the ball through with no play for him, no infielder known to be going for it is in front of it, by his estimate no other infielder (the pitcher included) can field it in the infield, and every infielder has shown what he is doing on this ball (`CALL_HEARD_S` after the first call at the soonest): first base, or third with a runner coming, is not left while an infielder may still field the ball. Toward home, unlike toward second or third, any infielder known to be going for the ball holds him at his base wherever the ball is, in front of that man or past him (revised 2026-11-21, Opus cutoff-home review P2-1): a man who has knocked the ball down and is running it down from behind may still make a play at first, and a real first baseman holds the bag until that is settled (seed 8 play 208: the first version sent the first baseman out at 2.28 s and first stood empty 1.8 s while the second baseman ran down a ball off his glove and fielded it 49 m out; 20 such plays in 120 games). Once out, a chase that becomes known takes him back to his base.
  - *The others by the convention* (`home_cutoff_by_convention`). Each infielder but the catcher who judges the ball through for himself (the ball down on the infield dirt, the likeliest throw home, nobody he knows of out on a cutoff yet, the cutoff man not known to be going for it, no infielder known to him to be going for it at all (as for the cutoff man himself, above); his own fresh read has it carrying beyond `INFIELD_BREAK_R` with no play for him; by his estimate no other infielder the ball has not yet gone by, the pitcher included, can field it in the infield) counts the cutoff man as out in his own working of the tables, at once. Once he has judged it so on this ball (`Fielder::through`) his read and estimate are not asked again, so the judgment does not come and go; a chase known to him ends it. Once someone is known to be on a cutoff or relay, the tables use what is known. He judges at the first call, at each hearing and, while he has no job, every tick (`through_jobs`: his own schedule, watching the ball go through); his teammates learn his job `CALL_HEARD_S` later, as any job.
  - *The coverage table with the first baseman cutting* (`home_order`): the second baseman covers first and the shortstop second; the pitcher covers no base and backs up home. If the shortstop is going for the ball or is himself on a cutoff, the second baseman covers second and first waits until the shortstop is free; a shortstop who is free takes second from a second baseman already on it, who goes to first once he knows the shortstop is on his way (before, on a ball to the left of centre the pitcher took first and the shortstop had nothing). Likewise the second baseman takes first from a pitcher who covered it while the first baseman was going for the ball, and the pitcher goes to back up home. And back: a first baseman who was out on the cutoff this play and has been replaced (a deep ball's relay man has taken over toward third; on a deep ball with a play at home he is not replaced but stays out behind the relay man, the double cut below), while the ball is out of the infield (loose beyond the infield dirt, or in an outfielder's hands; not thrown), takes first back from the second baseman, who is then free for second; otherwise the bases stand (Opus cutoff-home review P3-1: the first version applied to any first baseman with no job and reached nobody-on plays). And on: with the first baseman out on the cutoff home and the shortstop out on a cutoff or relay (or going for a ball out of the infield), a second baseman on first, put there while the shortstop was free, leaves it for second, the base the batter-runner goes for next (revised 2026-11-22, Opus liner-single re-review P3-1; [the relay hand-overs note](calibration/2026-11-22-relay-handovers.md)): he stayed on first, second open about 1.2 s, until the next re-call worked the table afresh (seeds 13/224, 93/113; second open on deep balls with a runner in scoring position 3.9 s → 2.2 s in 120 games). While the shortstop goes for a ball still in the infield he stays. The pitcher who judges the ball through backs up home at once, on the line from the `through_point` (`through_backup`); once someone is known to be fielding it, the backup table gives him the same job from there.
  - *Not changed:* the deep ball's relay man toward home (when the ball gets past the outfielder, the middle infielder nearer the relay spot goes out; if he was the man on second, second waits `CALL_HEARD_S` for the second baseman to see it); balls an infielder fields: the early cutoff and the judgment both hold while an infielder can field it or is known to be going for it. Once an outfielder is the known lead, `relay_job` sends the corner man out on the cutoff home from a loose ground ball only by the same infield gate (`infield_lets_go` toward home; revised 2026-11-22, Opus cutoff-home review P3-3, [the relay hand-overs note](calibration/2026-11-22-relay-handovers.md): without it he left his base while an infielder still went for the ball, seed 12 play 9, first open 0.4 s while the shortstop fielded a chopper 40 m out and threw the batter out at first; seed 191 play 125, third open 0.6 s). Where his teammates have already counted him out by the convention and the gate still holds him, his base has two men on it until they see him stay (3 plays over 0.5 s in 120 games, at most 0.82 s).
- *A ball in the air that will come down in front of an outfielder* (built 2026-11-21, `air_read`, `air_cutoff`, `single_of`; [the liner-single note](calibration/2026-11-21-liner-single-jobs.md); TASKS cases (a) and (d)). A line drive or blooper over the infield that no infielder can catch is a single as soon as the infielders see it will drop in, and they take the single's jobs then, as on a ground single, not when it comes down. Before this the conventions waited for the ball to come down; while it was up nobody acted on a single, and the deep-ball rule, reading the fielding point at the outfielder's feet, sent a middle infielder out as the relay man on a liner falling in front of a deep outfielder and back when it landed (in 120 games, 57 middle infielders' stretches with no job begun while such a ball was up with a runner in scoring position and first down in the dirt, 144 more first down in the shallow outfield; with nobody on, no cutoff man for second on any of 310 line-drive or fly singles first down in the shallow outfield).
  - *The judgment* (`air_read`), each infielder's own, but the catcher's: the ball is still up; no outfielder is known to him to have made a sure call on it (then a catch is expected); his read is recent (`HEAR_READ_AGE_S`, 1 s) and still has the ball in the air (an older read, or one that had it down by now, is not his view of the ball: a high fly read at the first call would otherwise be judged on a landing long past); it gives him no play (no catch, and no ball he fields inside `INFIELD_BREAK_R`); by his estimate (`seen_intercept` on his read) no other infielder the ball has not yet gone by catches it or fields it inside that range, by his read it comes down short of `RELAY_DEEP_M` (one coming down beyond is a catch or a deep ball, never a single in front of the outfielders, and the commonest ball in the air: it is asked first; with it the judgment costs about 4% of a game's run time, the standard run back to back with main, against 10% without); and the first outfielder to reach it does so only after it has come down, beyond `RELAY_FROM_M`, and not as a deep ball as `deep_ball` will judge it once it is down: fielded no deeper than `RELAY_DEEP_M`, or no more than `PAST_OF_M` deeper than the spot of the outfielder nearest where it comes down. He works it out at each look he takes at the ball (`LOOK_S`, 0.1 s) and on each new read of it, not every tick. Once made on this ball (`Fielder::air`) his read and estimates are not asked again, so it does not come and go as an outfielder's run changes the estimate. An outfielder who may still catch it (going for it without a sure call, or with a dive) does not stop it being judged a single when by the estimate he gets there late; one who can get there in time does.
  - *The cutoff man goes out at once* (`air_cutoff`): the single's cutoff man (toward second with nobody on, the middle infielder on the ball's side, `second_cutoff_man`; toward third with a runner on first, the shortstop; toward home with a runner in scoring position, the third baseman on a ball the left fielder will field, else the first baseman, for the outfielder known to be going for it or else the `through_point`'s, `single_of`), on his own judgment and the same infield gate as on a ground single (`infield_lets_go`: his partner not going for it, no infielder known to be going for it in front of it, toward home none at all; by his estimate no other infielder fields it in the infield; toward third or home every infielder known on this ball), to the cutoff spot of a throw through him (`through_spot`) on the line from the base to where he estimates it will be fielded. He keeps the job while the ball is up: it is his own, by his own judgment, and nobody else's choice takes it from him. Once the ball is down, the cutoff rule above takes over from where it is being fielded; on a single in front of the outfielder it is the same man, and it is not a deep ball (above).
  - *The others by the conventions*, as on a ground single: with a runner in scoring position each infielder but the catcher who judges it a single counts the cutoff man home as out in his own working of the tables (`home_cutoff_by_convention`, `through_jobs`: with the first baseman cutting, the second baseman to first and the shortstop to second; the pitcher behind home, `through_backup`); with a runner on first the second baseman takes second (`second_takes_over`). A judgment made while the ball was up stands after it comes down beyond the dirt (it is not asked again); an infielder known to him to be going for the ball suspends the conventions while that is known.
  - *A cutoff man who stays* (`single_cutoff_stays`, Opus liner-single review P1-1). Two infielders' reads of one ball can differ: one sees it dropping in, the other sees it carrying over the outfielder or caught, and stays on his bag. A man who has judged it a single counts the single's cutoff man as out only until he knows that man is staying: once the cutoff man is known to him, on this ball, to be on some other job than a cutoff or going for the ball, as known to him `CALL_HEARD_S` after the moment that man could first have gone (`could`: the later of when the counting man began counting him the single's cutoff man, `Fielder::cut`, where a runner seen going on or another outfielder taking the ball over changes who it is, and the moment every infielder was known on this ball, the first showing of each plus `CALL_HEARD_S`, the infield gate; his job may have been shown at any time on the ball, a cover is not shown again; the gate applies toward second as well as toward third and home), the conventions end for him on this ball (`Fielder::stays`) and he works the tables from what he knows; a man later known out on the cutoff is in them as known. In 120 games the conventions had two infielders on one bag with nobody on a cutoff or relay for more than 0.5 s on 32 plays (up to 3.2 s: seed 80 play 169, the first and second basemen both on first while the right fielder ran it down); now that lasts at most the 0.6 s it takes to see the cutoff man not go (the gate's 0.3 s and 0.3 s to see him). Asking instead that he was seen staying after the counting man's own judgment kept a man who judged it late, already out as the relay man, coming back to the bag for 0.3 s; and with no wait for the gate, a cutoff man who was only waiting for it was counted as staying.
  - *The deep ball's early relay man* (above: "early if his own read says he cannot get there before it lands") does not go out on a ball he judges will drop in as a single, unless the single's cutoff man is known to him to be staying (above: then nobody would be out for it), nor once he knows his partner to be out on a cutoff for it (the single's cutoff to second or third, which takes his place). A corner man known out on the cutoff home holds him in only on a ball his own read has coming down short of `RELAY_DEEP_M`, in front of the outfielders, where the single's cutoff man is the man for it; on one his read has coming down beyond it, the relay man and the cutoff man home are both out, as on any ball going deep (Opus liner-single review P2-1; before, a relay man who read it right went out, came back when the corner man was seen out, and went out again at the bounce: 7 such plays on deep balls with a runner in scoring position in 120 games; with the corner man's cutoff not counted at all, the relay man stayed out on balls that then fell in short, with first open while both were out: first uncovered while loose, runner in scoring position, shallow, 21.7 s against 8.6 s). When both are out and the ball comes down in the outfield short of `RELAY_DEEP_M`, the infield's call was split, and the fixed mark alone decides whether it is deep (`deep_ball`, below).
  - *A catch.* These jobs are right for a catch too: every base is covered (the bag a runner must tag up at or return to), and the cutoff man home is the man in line for the throw on a tag-up. A ball judged a single that an outfielder then catches is played as any catch, from his hands (the cutoff rule, deep beyond `RELAY_DEEP_M`). One new parameter, `PAST_OF_M` (above, "A deep ball"); `INFIELD_BREAK_R`, `RELAY_FROM_M`, `RELAY_DEEP_M` and `REACH` exist.
- *A man the ball has gone past keeps the cutoff* (`relay_job`, 2026-11-21). The rule that while the ball is loose a cutoff man plays it himself if his read gets him to it before the chaser could get to that spot applies only to an intercept inside `INFIELD_BREAK_R`. On the mirror of play 102 the shortstop's intercept in the outfield, sooner than the centre fielder's run to that same spot, had left him with no job at all.
- *The coverage table and the cutoff men* (revised 2026-11-21). When the tables are worked out at a call or re-call (`cover_table`), a man known to be out on a cutoff covers no base and keeps his job, as in the tables between calls (`cover_after_chasers`), and first and second are dealt as there when the first baseman is out as the cutoff home (`home_order`, above). Before, each once-a-second re-call dealt the bases as if the cutoff man were free, so his partner covering second (or the man covering first) lost his base and braked for 0.3 s until the cutoff man's job was known again. Third's order is the same in both: the third baseman, the shortstop, then the pitcher (revised 2026-11-22, [the relay hand-overs note](calibration/2026-11-22-relay-handovers.md)): the re-call's table had left the pitcher out, so with the third baseman and the shortstop both out he covered third between calls and went back to backing up home at each re-call, third open 0.3 s each time (seed 107 play 40: three times; seed 109 play 209).
- *The double cut* (built 2026-11-22, owner Q35 (c); `double_cut_job`, `deep_cut_by_convention`, `deep_jobs`; [the double-cut note](calibration/2026-11-22-double-cut.md)). On a ball in the gap or down a line that gets past the outfielders with a runner who may score, the standard alignment is a double cut: a middle infielder goes out as the relay man and the first baseman lines up behind him as the cutoff man for the throw home, whichever field the ball is in; the third baseman stays at third, the other middle infielder covers second, and first is left (the batter-runner is past it or going for second, and no throw from 80 m out can beat him back). The catcher stays home; the backups are the table's (the pitcher behind home, the far outfielder behind second).
  - *When.* A deep ball (`deep_ball`) that came down before anyone caught it (a hit: on a caught ball the relay man goes out alone, as before, and the runners must tag up, first included), being fielded by an outfielder (loose with an outfielder the known lead, or in an outfielder's hands) beyond `RELAY_FROM_M`, whose relay throw goes home (`relay_base` = home: a runner who was on base at the pitch on second or third, counted at the base he has reached, or one seen heading home). With a corner man known out on the cutoff home (the first baseman on the double cut; or a third baseman still known out on a single's cutoff home as the ball turns deep, until he is known back, and the double cut then follows), the throw stays home while a runner who was on base at the pitch is still on the bases (not out, not scored), moving or not, as on a single (`relay_base`'s hold, now on a deep ball too): the run from third scoring does not send the first baseman back while the runner from first is still coming. With no play at home (nobody on but the batter-runner, or a runner from first not yet at second) the relay man goes out alone toward third, as before.
  - *When the first baseman goes out* (owner Q36, decided 2026-11-22: (a), as built). When the ball has come down beyond the outfielders, a hit everyone has seen; not while a ball going deep is still in the air, when the relay man may already be out on his own read (a runner on first who went too far on a ball that is then caught is doubled off at first). Measured: a median 0.12 s after the relay man and 2.9 s before the outfielder's throw (the double-cut note §3, §6).
  - *Who and where.* The relay man is the deep ball's, as before (the middle infielder whose pre-pitch spot is nearer the relay spot), at the relay spot (`cut_spot`). The first baseman stands on the line from the relay man's spot to home, `through_spot` short of the plate (`CUTOFF_M` = 14 m, about 45 ft, or halfway on a short throw): where the relay man's throw through him, at his head, carries home on the fly or one hop (§5.5). While the outfielder's throw is in the air, he stays out only if it is coming to the man he knows to be the relay man, as he sees it fly: that man is the fielder (other than the thrower) nearest where it comes down through catching height (`throw_comes_to`), and he lines up from where that man is (revised 2026-11-22, Codex double-cut review P1-1: the first version read the throw's stored route, its base, receiver and relay flag, which are the thrower's intention, not what the first baseman sees; a radius of `LOW_THROW_MAN_M` = 4 m, the runners' "thrown to a man", missed a relay throw that came down 4.5 m short of him). While the relay man holds the ball, from where he holds it. A man going for the ball or holding it is not given it.
  - *What each man knows.* The deep call and the relay base are built only from what everyone has seen (where the ball came down, where it is and who is known to be going for it, each outfielder's pre-pitch spot, where the runners are, the jobs teammates have shown), so every fielder works the alignment out alike at the same tick, from the standard assignments, as on a single through the infield (`home_cutoff_by_convention`): the first baseman goes out at once by it (`double_cut_job`), and every other fielder counts the relay man and the first baseman as out in his own working of the tables at once (`deep_cut_by_convention`), not when their jobs are shown `CALL_HEARD_S` later; a man known to him to be going for the ball is not counted. The convention names all the cutoff men of a deep ball: a corner man known to be out on a single's cutoff home who is not its man (the third baseman) is counted as back at his base, and a middle infielder out on a single's cutoff to second or third who is not its relay man as free. The same holds for a deep ball's relay man with no play at home (toward third): his partner and the others count him out at once. Each fielder not going for the ball, not on a cutoff and not holding it re-works his own cover job when the convention as he works it changes (`deep_jobs`), the moment the ball is seen past the outfielders (a man who loses a cover takes his own next job, which may be a backup). No private state of the relay man or the first baseman enters a teammate's table: the relay man's own gates (his read, his intercept) decide only whether he himself goes, and on a ball down beyond `RELAY_DEEP_M` they do not hold him.
  - *Add a man, not trade one.* When a single becomes a deep ball (a ball that fell in front of an outfielder rolls past him), the first baseman already out on the single's cutoff home stays out and moves back onto the relay line, and the relay man goes out in addition (seed 23 play 83: before, the first baseman came back to first at 4.19 s, the second baseman ran from first to second and the shortstop out from second). On a ball the left fielder fields, the third baseman, out as the single's cutoff home, comes back to third and the first baseman goes out (seed 97 play 119: before, third had nobody assigned for 0.3 s while the shortstop, on third, went out as the relay man and the third baseman did not yet know it). A middle infielder covering second when his partner, out on the cutoff to third, is not the deep ball's relay man (a ball past the right fielder with a runner on first): the second baseman goes out and the shortstop takes second at once, by the convention, not 0.3 s later.
  - *The throws* (§5.5, the route). The outfielder with the ball has two men lined up in his throw home; the relay route goes to the nearer of the men lined up between him and the plate, the relay man, and the direct throw is the cuttable throw past the relay man's spot, as before. The relay man with the ball compares, by the same rule as any holder (§5.6, §5.5: believed time plus miss cost), a direct throw home, cuttable at the first baseman's spot, with one through the first baseman (his relay exchange `relay_s` and throw), and any other base a runner is visibly heading for. A throw that goes by the first baseman (the outfielder's throw anywhere but to the relay man, the relay man's anywhere but to him) ends his job, as for any cutoff man, and he takes his own next job at once: first, which nobody has (revised 2026-11-22, Opus and Codex double-cut reviews P2-1; before, he stood through the throw). *In practice* (throw physics, as in the test `the_double_cut_played_through`): from the relay man's spot 56 m out, for every arm that reaches the plate on the fly his own throw home beats the relay through the first baseman (two flights and the first baseman's exchange, 0.75 s at grade 50, against one flight). The relay through him is the quicker only for an arm that cannot reach the plate on the fly (about 22–25 m/s from 56 m), and then on a high throw of 2.6 s or more to the first baseman. So the first baseman in the line is mostly the man the throw home is kept low through, rarely a second relay.
  - *The coverage table* (a row of the table above: deep ball, a play at home). Home the catcher; third the third baseman; second the middle infielder who is not the relay man; first nobody; the pitcher backs up home (`home_order` with the first baseman out and the shortstop busy or the second baseman out: the tables already give these once both men are counted out).
  - No new parameter: `RELAY_DEEP_M`, `RELAY_FROM_M`, `CUTOFF_M`, `CUT_LINE_M` and `CALL_HEARD_S` exist.
  - *Not built.* A trail man (in some systems the second middle infielder goes out behind the relay man as a trailer for an overthrow) and the catcher's "Cut!" call (the first baseman never intercepts a throw aimed past him, §5.5).

**Calls, built 2026-11-12, revised 2026-11-13 (`Sim::call_value`, `assign_among`, `recall`):** each fielder judges from his own read. On a ball he believes he catches in the air with at least 0.3 s to spare (`CALL_SLACK_S` [E]) he goes for it, and he **calls it only when he is sure**: his time to spare must also cover `CALL_SURE_SIGMAS` = 1 [E] σ of his own read's depth error where the ball comes down, run off at his speed (`landing_depth_sigma`; a fielder knows how good his read is): from what he believes the ball is doing now he runs his own read (§5.1) once as it is and once with each of its four error draws at one σ, and adds up in quadrature how far each moves where he predicts it comes down, along his line of sight, with the spin he cannot see in the plate's share of his depth. Running his own physics carries drag, the plate cue's use of the same velocity he flies forward, and the coupled lateral and height draws. *Revised 2026-11-13 with the plate cue:* the first version used only the error of where the ball is now (σ₀ · d/30 m · 3 / √(1 + view/τ_read)), which was the whole story when that term was 22 m at first look; with the plate cue it is under a metre early, and what an outfielder is unsure of is the velocity. A straight-line sum of position and velocity terms (the cue's first version) missed that the plate position and the forward flight share one velocity error, and ignored drag (Codex plate-cue review #1); the per-draw version is within 15% of the sampled spread of his reads (test `a_fielders_doubt_matches_the_spread_of_his_reads`). **A call follows its caller's own reads (Codex fielder-communication fourth review P2-2, 2026-11-14; `Sim::refresh_call`):** whenever a man going for the ball reads it again (his replan every `REPLAN_S`, his close read within 4 m), he makes his call again from that read by the same rule: he stops calling a ball his latest read no longer has him sure of, or no longer has him catching in the air, and calls one he has become sure of. Teammates hear the change `CALL_HEARD_S` later, like any call, and a man who had given the ball up to it decides again when he hears it. *Before this the call was made only at the first read and the once-a-second re-call, and the re-call stops once the man known to be going is near the ball, so a caller kept a sure call his own fresh reads had withdrawn (seed 4 play 280: the centre fielder still showed a sure call at 6.19 s with his latest read having the ball down 0.6 s before he got there).* Sure calls rank outfielders over infielders, then by the priority table below (*revised 2026-11-14, live-play review P1-1:* the most time to spare, which nobody can hear, no longer decides). Until someone is sure, **everyone who believes he can catch it in the air goes for it**; when he hears a call that outranks his he peels off to his cover and backup jobs. Otherwise (grounders, balls nobody can catch in the air) each man goes if, by his own read and his own estimate of each teammate from where that man is and how he is moving, he fields it first, outfielders taking near-ties (0.15 s). **What a fielder knows of his teammates (2026-11-14, docs/calibration/2026-11-14-fielder-communication.md):** what he sees now (positions and motion, interim exact), what he knows before the pitch (attributes, alignment, the staff's tables), and a teammate's chase and call `CALL_HEARD_S` = 0.3 s [E] (`T_call` below) after it began; never a teammate's target, read or intercept time. *Why (2026-11-13, docs/calibration/2026-11-13-popup-read-and-calls.md):* the first version let an outfielder call any ball he believed he could catch, at his first look, over every infielder. At that look his depth read is about 25 m (one σ), so on shallow pop-ups he often called a ball he had misjudged, the infielder who had it stopped, and it dropped (pop-up hits .047 against MLB ≈ .02). Outfield priority now applies as it does on a field: to the outfielder who is sure, which for a ball in between means late, coming in on it. Once a second, while the man known to be going for it is far from it, the four fielders nearest **where everyone sees it being fielded** (on the ball's ground track nearest that man, not the ball's position now, which for a pop-up is over the infield) and every man going for it read it again and decide again (the chaser keeps the ball unless he hears a call that outranks his; `RECALL_MARGIN_S`, which compared two men's time to spare, is gone). The earlier rule, earliest intercept for everything, was a coin toss on fly balls (everyone catches when it comes down) and handed shallow pop-ups to outfielders who had misjudged them. Infielders break for a ground ball without a call only if their read has it coming down within 25 m **in the first second** (`GROUNDER_BOUNCE_S` [E]; an infield pop-up also lands within 25 m, seconds later), and they run at their own intercept (the rule used to leave them aimed at the base the cover step had just given them). **The first baseman and first base (2026-11-14, Opus infield-fixes review P1-1):** he breaks only for a ball whose intercept, by his own read, is on the first-base side of the line through the mound (x > 0), the side on which the pitcher's job is to cover first; on a ball to the left of the mound he goes to his bag. If by his own read the pitcher gets to it first (the pitcher who fields it cannot cover first), he stays with the bag; the pitcher, when his own read has the first baseman going for a ground ball, breaks for first at once. **Middle infielders and second base (2026-11-14):** a middle infielder whose own read has his partner fielding a ground ball in the infield covers second at once, whether or not it is in his own range (the man who does not field it covers), without waiting to see his partner go. His partner is left out of that judgment only when known to be on another base or out as the cutoff; a partner idle, backing up or covering second may field it (third review P2-1). **Giving up the chase (`chasers_see_the_ball_secured`):** a fielder still going for the ball who sees another fielder secure it (a visible fact, after his own `Reaction` time) gives up: the pitcher and first baseman go to first, the catcher home, the second baseman and shortstop second, the third baseman third, each only if nobody else is known to cover that base (a cover taken in the last 0.3 s is not yet known); anyone else pulls up where braking stops him. A man who has had the ball himself this play is left alone. Before this a secondary chaser ran on to his last intercept after the ball was fielded: on seed 250 play 109 the first baseman broke for a ball the pitcher fielded at (−5, 14), ran across the infield to (−17.5, 31.6), and the batter reached first with nobody there; over 300 games such no-throw infield hits with nobody near first rose from 62 to 157 with the first baseman in the break.

Coordination emerges from **calls**, not a central oracle [C]:
- Each fielder, from its own estimate, computes whether it can make the play. When its believed margin is positive, it "calls" the ball. A call reaches teammates after `T_call` = 0.3 s [E]. *Built 2026-11-14 (`CALL_HEARD_S`, `Sim::known`, `Sim::hear`):* a teammate's chase, whether called or not, and its call tier are known 0.3 s after it began; only sure calls rank against others. *Revised 2026-11-14 (Codex fielder-communication review P1-1; `Shown`, `Sim::seen`):* every job a man takes, not only a chase, is known 0.3 s after he takes it: the base he covers, the cutoff he goes out to, the backup he runs to. Each man works out the cover and backup tables **for himself**, from what he knows of his teammates and his own choice (his own chase, his own choice to cover second); he never uses a teammate's choice not yet seen or heard. The tables give everyone the same answer once the jobs are known; before that, two men can head for one bag for up to 0.3 s (then the man earlier in the table's order for it keeps it), and a base can wait for a man to be known free. A man's own stop is instant in his own table: an infielder called off a ball in the air takes his base at once if his table gives him one, and backs up the catch only if it does not. *Revised (Codex fielder-communication recheck P1-2):* a first version kept a man who had just stopped out of his own table until his stop was known; on fly balls several infielders start for, that left bases with nobody assigned for 0.6 s. The second baseman is dealt first base after second (he takes first only when second is someone else's). The same knowledge decides the base a chaser goes to when he sees the ball secured, the catcher's return to cover home, the backup of a throw, the man a holder throws to at a base, the cutoff man on the holder's route (recheck P1-1), and who takes the cutoff job (each man works it out for himself every tick, `update_relay`). A called-off infielder whose backup of the catch ends when the ball comes down reads it and takes his own job; his teammates are not re-called for it. A man who leaves or takes the cutoff job takes his own next job, and his teammates re-work theirs when the change is known (third review P1-1). **No man's job is ever re-worked because of another man's private state:** each refreshes on his own schedule or on public events (the first read, the once-a-second re-call, a new loose ball, the ball coming down, a ball secured, a throw, a change in what he knows). **The holder's call to a bag (Codex fielder-communication fourth review P1-1, 2026-11-14; `Fielder::asks`, `Sim::heed_requests`):** a holder who has chosen a throw and finds his man not yet at the bag waits for him and calls him to it ("two!", pointing at him). The call is published like any other and the man named goes to the bag only once he hears it, `CALL_HEARD_S` later, and only while that holder still has the ball and still calls for him; until then he does his own job, and when the call is withdrawn he keeps to the bag until his job is re-worked. *Before this the holder's private choice of base and receiver wrote the receiver's target in the same tick, with no throw and nothing shown (inherited from main; seed 1 play 195 sent the left fielder, known to be backing up second, 13 m to the bag; seed 8 play 121 pulled the second baseman off his cutoff line for eight ticks).* *Why a call, not the receiver's own reading:* the man is named by the holder's choice of base, which depends on the holder's own read of the race and his own set-up; nobody else can work that out from what they see. The cover table already puts a man on the bag whenever it is someone's job; this call is for the case it leaves the bag to whoever is nearest, and on a field that man is told, by voice or a point. Before a release nothing the holder decides privately reaches another man; a released throw is public. A test harness checks this on real games (`isolation_no_private_state_reaches_a_teammate_in_real_games`; widened after the fourth reviews, Codex P2-1, Opus P2-2): before every decision step of a live play (the first read, a new loose ball's read, the chasers' replans, the close and dive reads, the re-call, the end of a backup of a catch, a chaser seeing the ball secured, the receiver's read of a throw, the holder's choice, his call heeded, the backup of a released throw, the catcher's return home, hearing, the cutoff job) it changes one man's private state at a time (every job and backup base, his target, plan, call, his read cleared or replaced by another valid read, his error draws, planning clocks, a dive decided, a carry, a holder's set-up clocks and his call for a man to a bag; never what he has shown) and requires every other man's whole state and publications, the ball (a throw's whole state), the runners and the holder's options to come out the same. The holder's own change may decide his throw: after a release, which is public, it may differ. Rare steps and every call that changes a job are checked every time, others at a stride. A negative control restores the third review's cutoff gate and requires the harness to find it. Covers are re-worked when a job becomes known while the ball is loose or held (not while a throw is in the air). **Measured** (seeds 0–49, `sim/crates/bb_game/examples/coverage_trace.rs`, `analysis/coverage_gaps.py`; the calibration note §7's current summary, on `3b7cb38` on main `57f12b7`): first went without an assigned cover for more than 0.3 s 3 times (main: never), two after a throw got away at first and one on a foul pop caught for the out, the longest 1.02 s; second 15 times (main 11), longest 1.33 s, mostly both middle infielders breaking for the same ball before they hear each other; third 31 times (main 68); two covers on one base and nobody chasing, never.
- Conflicts resolve by a priority table (CF > corner OF; OF > IF on balls in between; SS > 3B on balls to the left, etc. [data file]). A fielder who hears a higher-priority call peels off to back up. *Built 2026-11-14 (`CALL_PRIORITY` [E]):* outfield CF > LF, RF; infield SS > 2B > 3B > 1B > C > P; OF over IF is the call tier. *Revised 2026-11-14 (Codex fielder-communication review P2-2; `outranks`):* the left and right fielders share a priority, so between their two sure calls the lower position number keeps the ball (LF, 7, over RF, 9), a convention both know before the pitch; the other peels off and, as the nearest free outfielder, backs him up. Before this both kept going after hearing each other.
- *Where the ball is being fielded, as everyone sees it (`Sim::fielding_point`, 2026-11-14):* the holder; while it is loose, the point on its ground track nearest the man known to be going for it. Cutoffs, backups and the side for second base's cover use it (the side from the ball's heading until someone is known to be going). Backups and the relay wait until someone is known to be going: 0.3 s when a man goes at the first read, 0.6 s when every man at first leaves the ball to another. **A ball every man has left to another (Codex fielder-communication recheck P1-2; `fallback_chaser`, `public_rank`):** once every man is known not to be going for it, one man goes, by an order every fielder works out alike from what all can see: the soonest to field the ball, each man's earliest standing intercept (or dive) of the ball as all see it now run on with the agreed model (its present position and velocity, the typical spin for how it is moving, the fielders' own flight and roll physics, no bounce scatter: its descent and where it stops), from where he is and how he moves, at his known speed after his known reaction. *A dead heat (revised 2026-11-14, Opus fielder-communication fourth review P3-1):* the intercept scan's grid makes exact ties, most often on pop-ups (one over the mound tied the pitcher, catcher and first baseman); a tie goes by the priority everyone knows before the pitch, an outfielder over an infielder, then `CALL_PRIORITY`, then the lower position, so the pitcher is the last choice, as on a field, where the pitcher is called off pop-ups. It used to go to the lower position, the pitcher. The same order breaks ties in which of two men going for the ball keeps going (`publicly_before`). *Revised (Codex fielder-communication third review P1-2):* the first version measured each man's run to the ball's ground line, unbounded and blind to when the ball gets there, so a man on that line far past where a slow ball stops, or the pitcher under an outfield fly's line, ranked first. A man known to be covering first is left out, so first stays covered. Nobody else moves; his chase is known 0.3 s later. *Before this, every man with a recent read decided again with every teammate known not to be going left out of his comparison, so all nine went, then all stopped, every 0.6 s, with the bases open for up to 4.8 s.* **A chase is not dropped only because more men have become known to be going:** a man already going for a ball nobody catches in the air stops only for a teammate known to be going who, by his own estimate, gets there first **and** is ahead of him in that shared order; the man first in the order is never stopped by the others. A first baseman going for a ground ball goes back to his bag when the ground-ball rule leaves it to someone else, or when the pitcher is known to be going for it and is ahead of him in the order. *Approved by the owner 2026-11-14, to be built after this change merges, on its own branch:* a teammate is seen to go before his call is heard when he visibly runs at the ball: a chase is known at the earlier of `T_call` and a motion-onset time for seeing a man break toward the ball (a new [E] parameter, to be sourced from visual motion-onset detection data). It would shorten the 0.6 s case; the recovery above does not depend on it.
- **A ground ball through the infield (`Sim::hear`, 2026-11-17).** When every outfielder is known to have left this ball and the known chasers are infielders, one outfielder goes for it, provided the ball has bounced and the shared prediction carries it beyond `INFIELD_BREAK_R`. He is the first outfielder in the same shared order who is known to be free of a base or cutoff job. If he is still free of those jobs and holds a recent read of this ball (`HEAR_READ_AGE_S` = 1.0 s), he runs at that read's intercept: his read sets where he runs, not whether. He stays only if he has heard a sure call. The rule is looked at only at a hearing, when a chase or call becomes known, so in practice at the first hearing (0.55 s after contact in 97% of cases in the Fable review's probe). It fires on about one ball in play in twenty, and an infielder fields nine of those ten: the outfielder is then backing up the infielder, as he would after going at the first read. *Not covered (Fable review P2-1): the same misplay on a ball still in the air when the infielder's chase becomes known* (seed 43 play 74). In the owner's saved play (seed 65147 play 10), the centre and right fielders each read a chopper as the other's (§5.1), so both stayed. The first baseman broke for it, which kept the all-fielders fallback from acting as the ball rolled through the right side. The right fielder now cuts it off short of the warning track.
- Two fielders who both commit before a call arrives can converge, and the world resolves the result physically (both reaching, a collision later). Rare mix-ups emerge.
- **Where the holder throws, built 2026-11-12, revised 2026-11-13 (`holder_act`):** for each running runner he judges the chance of an out at the base the runner is heading for, p = Φ((runner's time − ball's time)/0.12 s), from what he can see. Plays with p > 0.3 are scored p² × w and the highest wins. Squaring p makes a sure out beat an uncertain play on a lead runner. With fewer than two out, w weights the lead runner: 0.9 / 1.0 / 1.1 / 1.3 for first / second / third / home. With two out every out ends the inning, so w is 1.0 for a force and 0.9 for a tag play (`TWO_OUT_TAG_W` [E]). A force needs no tag, and no run can score on a third-out force (OBR 5.08(a)), so an equally sure tag loses to the force. A sure tag still beats a doubtful force. Before this, on seed 7475 play 26 (runners on first and third, two out, a chopper to the pitcher), the pitcher threw home for a tag play instead of taking the force at first.
- **Coverage and cutoffs** come from situation tables (base-out state × ball direction → who covers which base, who cuts off). These are data, authored from standard baseball defensive assignments, and are beliefs the coaching staff holds.

### 5.8 First base

- **Foot on the bag:** reach is a volume anchored at the base, extended by stretching. Stretching takes 0.25 s [E] and gains up to 0.6 m [E] of reach, which shortens the throw's effective arrival time.
- **Scoop:** a capture of a short-hopped throw, using k_hop.
- **Off-bag decision:** if the predicted throw is outside the anchored reach, the first baseman leaves the bag to catch it and attempts a tag if the runner is close [E rule: when believed P(tag) > 0.3], otherwise concedes the base.

### 5.9 Positioning

Pre-pitch infield positions are physical inputs set before each pitch from league-average Baseball Savant fielder-positioning data (2024 MLB season, `Shaded: No` / `shift=0` standard non-shifted alignment under the 2023+ two-infielders-per-side rule, PA-weighted means of Savant's full-precision Cartesian fields `(avg_norm_start_pos_x, avg_norm_start_pos_y)` with `attempts=1` converted to polar `(hypot, atan2)`; [Baseball Savant Fielder Positioning](https://baseballsavant.mlb.com/visuals/fielder-positioning)). Coordinates are polar from home plate: straight-line depth in feet (`0.3048 m/ft`) and horizontal spray angle in degrees (`0°` straight to CF along `+Y`, `-45°` toward the 3B line, `+45°` toward the 1B line).

| Alignment (`season=2024`, `shift=0`) | `1B` depth / angle | `2B` depth / angle | `3B` depth / angle | `SS` depth / angle | Source filter |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| vs. RHB, Standard (`firstBase=0`, `n=53,775`) | 114.1 ft / +29.6° | 152.9 ft / +6.8° | 122.0 ft / -36.4° | 148.3 ft / -18.3° | `batSide=R&firstBase=0&shift=0` |
| vs. RHB, Double-play (`firstBase=1`, `n=17,792`) | 88.0 ft / +40.5° | 148.7 ft / +6.7° | 118.8 ft / -36.5° | 144.8 ft / -17.3° | `batSide=R&firstBase=1&shift=0` |
