The rule makes a runner halfway to a base the ball is already at turn back where before he ran on into the tag.
- *Looking again from a base* (`runner_relook`, `base_relook`; 2026-11-23, TASKS §1, [throw-race note §7](calibration/2026-11-19-throw-race.md#7-open)). A runner standing on a base he chose to hold (his goal, at rest on the bag) looks again on the same two events as a runner between bases: a ball secured by a fielder, or a throw released, since his last look. Before this he never decided again while the ball was held or thrown, so a runner holding on third stayed while the right fielder threw from the wall to second. The look is his ordinary decision at a base (`runner_decide`), with nothing new in it: from rest, his own legs to the next base against his own read of the ball there plus the tag, the timing draw, the caution and the run values of §6.3. A throw in the air is his `ThrownRead`, so a throw to another base reaches his only by a second throw from there (`RELAY_READ_S` and a typical throw), from the relay man it comes down at (below), or on the way when its track passes his bag; a ball held is the holder read with the defense's expected play. If he goes and the play changes again (the throw is caught at second and the man there can still beat him), he is between bases and the look above may send him back. *A quick return throw.* When his look brings a throw down at the base he stands on, or past his bag where it can be taken (and not at a relay man), the man taking it there has his next base a short throw away and nobody else to play: the runner gives the ball to his next base that man's quickest, `QUICK_RETURN_S` = 0.27 s (a hurried release, `RUSH_MIN_FRAC` 0.6, of a grade-50 infielder's set exchange on a bag, 0.7 × 0.65 s: the fielders' own model as league knowledge, no new parameter) and a typical throw, where a throw to another base gets `RELAY_READ_S` 0.6 s. *Why:* measured before it (300 games on each standard set), 8 runners from second were put out at third, every one after leaving on a throw from the outfield corners or deep center to second that the man there caught and rushed to third in 0.28–0.47 s. Keeping a runner on every throw to his own base instead (tried) also stopped the runner who takes third on a slow throw to second that the man there cannot beat him on: 73 of the 84 who went from second had been safe, and with that rule only 6 went. A runner ahead who has not cleared the next base leaves him waiting on that man (the held-base rule: he decides when it is cleared), as at any base. A forced runner is never standing on a base he holds: he has gone. A runner tagging up, holding partway, retouching, turning back for the runner ahead, set for his start or in the batter's overrun keeps his own rule, and a look waits for any catch call he has not yet seen. Each look is a new decision with its own draws (sub-indices 2n and 2n + 1), as every look: no new stream. *When he looks at a throw (review 2026-11-23, P2-1).* Not at its release: he watches it for his read time, `RUNNER_READ_DELAY_S` (0.5 s, the same as his first look at a batted ball), since nothing makes a runner on his bag commit at the release, where his read of how the throw is moving is at its worst; a catch before then is new, and he decides on that. And a throw his look carries over or near the base in front of him (its track passes within `CUT_LINE_M` of that bag, or of the man visibly covering it, at any height: that man is in the throw, as a cutoff man is) he watches until it gets there (`throw_watch`; that look is spent), and decides then on a fresh look, or on the catch if it is taken first: whether it is taken there or gets by is what decides his race, and he cannot tell it earlier. *Why:* all three runners put out from first in the first full measurement had gone at the release on a throw to the man covering second that his look carried past second (a speed read 2.8–2.9σ high from right field, a 1.4σ read from deep center, where second and home lie on one line), naming third or home and timing second as a second throw back, 1.5–2.5 s later than the ball was there. Decided at the release, the review's fixture from deep center sends him on 44 of 4,096 draws; with the rule, none. *What it does not do:* a runner holding stands on the bag, so he leaves from rest (third to home in 4.10 s for the league runner); a real runner on third walks off a few steps while the ball is held, and that walking lead is not modelled. From the bag he beats a throw to second only when it is long and slow: from deep right-center (52, 92) at the right fielder's average 40.5 m/s the world has the ball home and tagged 4.23 s after release at the quickest exchange, against his 4.10 s.
- *A throw to the relay man* (2026-11-23). A throw his look brings down at a man (within `LOW_THROW_MAN_M` of where it comes down, not behind it) who visibly has the cutoff job (`known_job`: `Relay` for any base) goes on from that man: the ball reaches each base `RELAY_S_50` (the league relay man's exchange) and a typical throw from where he stands after it comes down there, or earlier on the way when its track passes the bag. Before this he timed it as a second throw from the base nearest that man: a throw from deep right-center to the cutoff man for home at (26, 46) came down nearest second and was home 3.34 s after release by that read, against the world's 4.09 s through the cutoff man; from the relay man he reads 4.01 s. Only the shown job counts: a man merely standing off the bags where a noisy read brings the throw down (a second baseman at his position short of the bag he covers) is no relay. *Tests* (`sim/crates/bb_game/src/live.rs`): a runner held on third goes on a throw from deep right-center to second at a weak 35 m/s arm, where the world has the ball home and tagged at the quickest 0.2 s or more after him; he stays on a throw from right field to the cutoff man lined up with home and on a throw to second from shallow right, where the world beats him with half a second to spare; a runner held on second stays on a throw from the right-field corner to second at a 37 m/s arm, where the world has the ball at third with the tag on before him at the receiver's hurried exchange, and takes third on the same throw at 32 m/s, where it cannot, and on a throw home from there; a runner held on second with the lead runner standing on third waits on him, and the batter walking back from his overrun takes no look; the throw to the relay man is read from him within 0.1 s of the world at release and later in its flight, where the base-nearest read was 0.75 s early. All fail on the code before.
- *Tests* (`sim/crates/bb_game/src/live.rs`): the destination is read right from left, center and right field to second, third and home, at release and later in flight; a throw from center to second is timed at its flight to second and one home on the fly at its flight home (the other base a relay later); a throw passing a base low is caught there on the way and not one lobbed over it or wide of it; the batter at first with a throw from center to second in the air stays at first; a runner 8 m past second with the third baseman holding the ball on third goes back, one forced from first does not, and one with a trailing runner standing on second does not go back to it; the turn is a stop (no backward step in the first tick, a 4.08 m drift on, arrival within three ticks of the estimate) and a second look while returning counts it; Codex's late-arrival probe leaves no two on a base and a trailing runner who can stop gives way; the review's three throw counterexamples are read right, and a sweep of 3 origin rows by 3 bases by 3 arms at four points of the flight projects the correct destination for each known observation; ten real games have no `two_runners_on_base`. They fail on the code before. The play itself cannot be replayed (the game moves under any change to a runner), so the first test above is its situation rebuilt.

---

## 7. AI Manager

Uses the identical command API (data-formats §6). v1 policy, detailed in M6:
- **Win expectancy and leverage** from the learned tables (§8).
- **Pitching changes:** uses only what a manager can observe: pitch count, the visible fatigue band, times through the order, matchups, the three-batter minimum (D17), and bullpen readiness.
- **Tactics** (steal, bunt, hit-and-run, intentional walk, alignment) chosen by comparing table-estimated run or win values, shifted by a **personality vector** [C]: analytics-driven, small-ball, quick-hook, and so on as weights on these terms.
- Emits `ManagerNote` snapshots with its believed win probability and reason (data-formats §5.5).

---

## 8. Learned Beliefs

Tables are agent beliefs (PLAN §8.5, D1). They are built offline from the sim's own Monte Carlo output, shipped as data files, and referenced by hash in `ProducerInfo.belief_tables_hash` (data-formats §3).

| Table | Key | Value | Used by |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `RE` run expectancy | base-out state (24) | expected runs to end of inning | Everyone (ΔRE) |
| `CountRV` | count × base-out | run value of a ball, a strike | Batter take value, pitcher |
| `PitchRV` | pitch type × location bucket (13 × 13) × count group × batter class × surprised | expected run value | Pitcher selection |
| `SwingW` | aim error (Δx, Δz, Δt bins) × location bucket × count group × batter class | expected run value of the swing | Batter swing decision |
| `WE` win expectancy | inning × half × score diff × base-out | P(win) | Manager, leverage |
| `LI` leverage index | same as `WE` | leverage | Composure, manager |
| `ThrowOut` | throw distance × runner speed class × fielder arm class | P(out) | Fielder decisions (prior on σ_FIQ estimates) |

**Bootstrap (M3–M4):** hand-written heuristics and a public-style RE/WE table [E: standard run-environment values]. `SwingW` starts as a Gaussian contact-probability model over aim error with hand-set run values.

**Learning loop (M6):**
1. Run the cage and full games with the current tables.
2. Fit each table from the logged outcomes (event logs plus the per-pitch beliefs recorded in `SwingRecorded.batter_read`).
3. Replace the tables; repeat until the change in each table is below a tolerance (about 3–5 rounds expected).
4. Regenerate per league environment (M7).

Tables must be **smooth and monotone where physics says so** (more break never helps a hitter guessing the other type). Learning fits use shrinkage toward the previous round so AIs cannot lock onto an exploit of another AI's blind spot.

---

## 9. Belief Snapshots (fills data-formats §5.5)

Each model emits these only when a timeline is requested, and recording must not change behavior ([data-formats §5.5](data-formats.md)).

| Snapshot | Emitted by | Fields |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `BatterBelief` | §3.3 every observation; §3.5 at commit | per hypothesis: type, w_k, s_k mean (x, z, t), Σ_k (6 floats). At commit: aim point, P̃(strike), EV_swing, EV_take, θ_dir, decision |
| `BatterAdjust` | §3.6 each tick from commit to cutoff | updated aim point, executed θ, adjustment rate saturation flags, check-swing state |
| `PitcherIntent` | §2.4 | chosen (k, target); top 3 alternatives with V; π̃ (the pitcher's model of the batter's expectations); directive |
| `FielderRead` | §5.1–5.3 each re-plan | estimated intercept point, σ_e along/across the line of sight, intercept time, role (primary/backup/cover/cutoff), call state |
| `ThrowDecision` | §5.6 | options with P̂(out) and value; chosen option |
| `RunnerDecision` | §6.3 | option, P̂(safe), ΔRE terms, coach signal |
| `ManagerNote` | §7 | believed WE, decision, reason code |

---

## 10. Attribute-to-Parameter Mappings

Defaults for an MLB-like population. **All values [E]** unless marked; `calibration-targets.md` (P3) owns the population distributions and their tuning. For "less is better" rows, the 80 and 20 columns use the ρ rule of §1.2.

| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |

| `BatSpeed` | sweet-spot speed at the nominal contact instant, any aim (§3.6 v2) | m/s | 32.0 (71.6 mph; Statcast average 71.5) | σ 1.25 [R: SD of batters' true mean competitive bat speed, 2.81 mph, Savant 2025 sample (SD of 216 means 2.90 less sampling error 0.72); owner ruling D19, 2026-11-14; was 1.1] | 35.75 | 28.25 |

| `Adjustability` | v_adj | m/s | 0.6 | σ 0.15 | 1.05 | 0.15 |
| `Speed` | v_max (sprint speed) | m/s | 8.23 (27 ft/s) [S: Statcast average] | σ 0.46 | 9.61 | 6.85 |
| `Acceleration` | τ_a | s | 1.00 | ρ 1.08 | 0.79 | 1.26 |
| `Agility` | a_lat | m/s² | 7.0 (built 2026-11-12, §5.3) | σ 0.8 | 9.4 | 4.6 |

| `ReadSkill` | read position σ at 30 m (outfield; infield 0.3); read velocity σ per axis, fraction of speed; τ_read (built 2026-11-15, `FielderAttrs::with_read`) | m; —; s | 3.5; 0.04; 0.6 | ρ 1.15 for the two σs (1.06 until 2026-11-17); τ_read ρ 1.06 (was 1.2, never used) | 2.30; 0.026; 0.50 | 5.32; 0.061; 0.71 |

Interim outfielder values (owner accepted 2026-11-11, `FielderAttrs::average`; docs/calibration/2026-11-11-fielding-planner.md): reaction 0.35 s, top speed while chasing 8.2 m/s, initial read error 2.5 m at 30 m (re-fitted after the left-field index fix; the owner had accepted 0.40 / 8.0 / 3.0). They were fitted to line-drive and fly-ball hit rates and stand in for the atlas perception of §5.1 until it is built; the grade-20 and grade-80 columns above are for the infield value.
| `PitchCraft` | w | 0–1 | 0.5 | σ 0.15 | 0.95 | 0.05 |
| `Composure` | C | 0–1 | 0.5 | σ 0.15 | 0.95 | 0.05 |
| `Adaptation` | η | per pitch seen | 0.15 | σ 0.04 | 0.27 | 0.03 |

Notes:
- Height, mass, and handedness are not graded.
- **`ReadSkill` (2026-11-15; docs/calibration/2026-11-15-outfield-read.md).** How well a fielder reads a ball in the air (§5.1). One grade scales his read's position error, velocity error and τ_read by ρ 1.06 per 10 grades [E]. The ρ is small because the catch rate is steep in the read error: on the routine flies of the note (Statcast ≥ 95% plays), an outfield all at grade 20 catches .917, grade 50 .960, grade 80 .983 (seeds 0–119; ρ 1.4 had grade 20 at .54). *Refitted 2026-11-17 (docs/calibration/2026-11-17-track-bias.md):* that steepness came from reads that could not correct a persistent error (the snapshot read repeated its fixed draws; the tracking read took them as fresh and grew sure of a wrong line). On the honest read (§5.1, the persistent error as a state) ρ 1.06 left grade 20 at .971, no worse than grade 50's .967, so the two read σs now take ρ 1.15 (grade 20 .915, grade 50 .967, grade 80 .984 when fitted; .934 / .979 / .986 with the full Jacobian; seeds 0–99; ρ 1.25 had grade 20 at .815); τ_read keeps ρ 1.06 (the tracking read does not use it). The owner's band for such a play is 90–99% by fielding ability (2026-11-15); the read is not the only thing between grade 80 and 99% (the glove, the dive rule). Every rostered player is still grade 50; ratings per player wait for the population generator. This is not a fielding-error rating: a ball reached and dropped is A17's, later.
- `Discipline` maps to two parameters with opposite signs: a high grade sharpens (c_D up) and removes the swing bias (b_D down).
- Bounded quantities (`PitchCraft`, `Composure`) clamp to [0, 1].
- **Throwing ratings (backlog A3, 2026-11-13).** `FielderAttrs::with_throwing(strength, accuracy, transfer)` gives a fielder the three grades: `ArmStrength` moves his top throw speed about his position's average (per-position means from Statcast, below), `ArmAccuracy` sets his direction σ, `Transfer` scales his position's catch-to-release (0.65 s infield, 0.85 s outfield, interim). Every rostered player is still grade 50; drawing ratings per player waits for the population generator (calibration-targets).
- **Arm strength by position (2026-11-14; `bb_game::play::ARM_MEAN_MPH`).** The grade-50 `ArmStrength` of a position's fielder is that position's measured mean, not one number for all infielders and one for all outfielders.
  - *What Statcast measures.* Savant's arm-strength leaderboard is, per player, the mean speed of his *max-effort* throws, defined by Savant and MLB's glossary as his hardest throws: the top 1% for a first baseman, 5% for the other infielders, 10% for an outfielder (minimums 100 / 75 / 50 throws), taken at the release point (the glossary notes that outfielders' running starts can put their throws above a pitcher's). The league positional means (2026 leaderboard, docs/calibration/2026-11-14-sources.md) are in mph: 1B 79.3, 2B 79.1, 3B 84.9, SS 86.1, LF 87.4, CF 89.6, RF 90.7.
  - *What the sim's value is.* `ArmStrength` is "max throw speed" (§10), and every set throw is released at it (`THROW_EFFORT` 1.0, §5.5; speed noise 3%; hurried throws less). So the sim's number is the speed of a full-effort throw, which is what Statcast's hard-throw mean also describes: a thrower's hard throws, not his routine ones. The conversion is therefore the unit change only, × 0.44704 m/s per mph (CF: 89.6 × 0.44704 = 40.05 m/s), with no discount for "typical" throws and no uplift to a record throw. The table below is that conversion.
  - *What stays an estimate [E].* (1) That a hard-throw mean equals a set, full-effort throw: Statcast's selection is of a player's own hardest throws in his position's throwing context (the fraction differs by position group), so a set throw is probably a little below it, and an outfielder's running start (crow hop) adds speed the sim's stationary release does not have; the sim's outfield values may therefore be on the high side by a few mph. (2) Pitcher and catcher have no value in the table (the catcher's arm is on a separate leaderboard; the 2017 HitTrax figure of 81.8 mph, 36.57 m/s, is a medium-confidence cross-check only), so they keep the previous 36 m/s. (3) Position means are not player distributions: the spread across players of one position (σ 2.2 m/s in §10) is unchanged and unsourced.

| Position | Statcast mean, mph | Sim `ArmStrength` at grade 50, m/s |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| P, C | none (kept) | 36.00 |
| 1B | 79.3 | 35.45 |
| 2B | 79.1 | 35.36 |
| 3B | 84.9 | 37.95 |
| SS | 86.1 | 38.49 |
| LF | 87.4 | 39.07 |
| CF | 89.6 | 40.05 |
| RF | 90.7 | 40.55 |

- PLAN §7.1 lists `PopTime` as a catcher attribute. Under §4 it is derived from `Transfer` and `ArmStrength`. PLAN §7.1 is updated to match.

---

## 11. Validation

Each mechanism gets tests that need no full game. Monotonicity tests feed `sim-cli sensitivity` (PLAN §13.3).

**Unit and analytic**
- Release point §2.2: the worked example; mirror symmetry between right and left-handers.
- Aiming §2.3: intended pitches hit their targets to < 1 mm for every arsenal type.
- Spin construction §2.1: round trip through data-formats §9.
- Batter filter §3.3: with zero observation noise and a single hypothesis, the posterior mean converges to the true crossing; with two identical hypotheses, weights stay equal.
- Swing inverse §3.6: noise-free swings put the sweet spot on the aim point to < 1 mm.
- Capture §5.4: the closed-form P(miss pocket) matches Monte Carlo for fixed σ_g.
- Movement §5.3: the home-to-first check.
- Execution isolation §1.1: `bb_agents/tests/exec_isolation.rs` scans the `bb_agents` sources for decision code naming `Executed*` types or importing `motor` (a compile-fail test is not possible inside one crate, see §1.1), and a self-test plants each violation kind to prove the scanner flags it.

**Monotonicity (property tests)**
- More `Command` → smaller mean miss distance. More `BatSpeed` → higher mean exit velocity. More `Vision` → smaller aim error. More `SpinRecognition` → fewer swings at the wrong hypothesis. More `Discipline` → lower chase rate. More `Hands` → fewer misplays. More `Speed` → shorter home-to-first. More `Composure` → smaller noise growth with leverage.
- Pitch tunneling: holding the release state fixed, a slider whose path matches the fastball's longer gets more whiffs from the same batter.
- Adaptation: whiff rate against the same pitcher falls with plate appearances (times through the order).

**Behavioral bands (checked in M3–M5, values in `calibration-targets.md`)**
- Pitcher release-point distributions in public ranges (release height 5.5–6.5 ft, side 1–3 ft).
- Zone rate, chase rate, whiff rate, contact rate, and pitches per plate appearance.
- Routine-play success > 98%; error rate in envelope.

---

## 12. Performance Budget

PLAN §13.4: ≤ 200 ms per headless game on one core, ~300 pitches and ~70 balls in play.

| Component | Budget per game [E] | Measured 2026-10-31 | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Game-start precompute (batter tables, atlas lookups, aim corrections) | 10 ms | 8 ms | 18 scouting builds, seeded from the cached aim solves (was 23 ms) |
| Pitch selection | 15 ms | < 1 ms | |
| Aiming (2–3 flights per pitch) | 20 ms | 13 ms | One full-step flight plus coarse search flights, with release execution (was 21 ms) |
| Pitch flight (world) | 10 ms | 12 ms | |
| Batter perception and decision | 10 ms | ~25 ms | Filter updates with an analytic observation Jacobian (10 ms), decisions (7 ms), aim points from the nominal path's own depth crossing (3 ms); was 71 ms with a finite-difference Jacobian and a 1 ms path march |
| Swing and contact (world) | 15 ms | 8 ms | |
| Fielding, running, coordination (30 Hz) | 60 ms | ~70 ms | Live plays at 60 Hz, batted-ball flight and rules stepping included. Fielder predictions ~38 ms (2,563 per game; agents fly their predictions at 0.1 s, within a few µm of the world's step); intercept scans 4 ms. Was 114 ms |
| Batted-ball flight and bounces (world) | 20 ms | (above) | |
| Rules, scoring, event log | 10 ms | ~20 ms | Remainder: game loop, event log, timelines |
| **Total** | **≈ 170 ms** | **≈ 155–170 ms** | One thread, 259 pitches and 66 balls in play (seed 3); best of five runs, since timings vary widely with machine load. Within the 200 ms budget |

M3 measures the real numbers with `criterion`. If a component exceeds its budget, cache or table it first; never simplify the model in a way that changes behavior without a decision-log entry.
